C5.7 — supply connection and service access

The first brake now has two body-to-truck supply hoses with fittings, supported straight leads, sliding guides, mounting hardware, proposed welds and a rounded stand-off guard. The body bracket attaches to the actual C5 bolster. The local air-support assembly is relocated 50 mm along +X; its base is extended over the unchanged HSS attachment and its two free hose curves are regenerated. The truck connection joins those relocated couplings through catalog-sourced Parker 2202P-6-6 elbows. Of 199 C56 parts, 156 retain the same geometry, 40 are relocated and 3 are revised; 80 new parts are added. All 105 primary mounting-section parts retain identical BREP serializations in the independent comparison.

The movement screen combines 45 body postures with 32 suspension/wear postures (1440 discrete configurations). Minimum supply-curve bend radius is 51.83 mm against the 45 mm catalog value; the regenerated local hoses reach 66.68 mm. Revised local hoses are checked anew against all relevant current fixed, primary-moving and body hardware; no earlier C56 hose clearance is inherited. The checked nominal and moving interactions have no positive modeled intersections. These sampled poses do not establish continuous clearance, manufacturing allowance or hose life.

Hose twist remains unresolved. Rotation-minimizing frame transport finds up to 21.08° change in geometric end-clock mismatch for the supply hoses and 14.49° for the revised local hoses. This is a compatibility diagnostic, not a measured material twist or stress. Assembly swivels are not live rotary joints. The route needs hose-manufacturer flex validation or further redesign before acceptance; a clear centreline and adequate bend radius do not close this hold.

The straight socket path still hits the existing mount. The proposed 30 mm pull, 60 mm sideways shift and continued pull to 90 mm was checked as six continuous exterior swept volumes, with a minimum modeled gap of 31.35 mm. Full 0–75 mm chamber withdrawal was checked continuously, with 29.94 mm minimum modeled gap. Local complete hoses, guide tops and chamber nuts are removed in the access state; the new supply lines remain installed. Actual caliper cavities, nut seating, socket internals, hand/ratchet space, pad extraction and final parking-release access remain unconfirmed.

No Webb or ZF reply was found in the recorded check. No supplier compatibility, circuit mapping, maximum force or final brake-mount sizing changed. The existing bearing-carrier, keyed-hub, torque-link, retained-pin and frame sections remain linked as C56-D01/D02/D03. Upstream coach controls/trainlines, anti-compounding and additional brake locations remain open. No other brake station was copied.

Geometry notes

C5.7 body-to-truck air connection

This revision adds two candidate supply hoses and their installed supports to the first brake installation. It also shifts the existing fixed local air assembly 50 mm toward positive X to clear the body adapter through combined yaw and tilt. The air baseplate is extended, and the two local free hose curves are regenerated. All 105 selected primary mounting parts remain unchanged: no axle, bearing, hub, caliper carrier, reaction link or frame member is resized. Other brake stations are not copied.

Installed path

The body support bears against the actual positive-X face of the retained C5 body bolster at X50.8 mm. Its six-millimetre backing plate occupies Z690–780 mm. A six-millimetre overhead shelf and two side gussets carry the twin bulkhead anchor plate at X528–534 mm, with ports at Z740 mm. The body ends point toward negative X; their 70 mm exposed straight leads pass through upside-down STAUFF smooth guide blocks before reaching X390 mm. The shelf sits above the hoses at Z760.75–766.75 mm. The bracket spans Y−260 to −105 mm; its underside gussets sit outside the guide and weld-base envelopes. Its overhang and welds require air-line loads, material and fatigue qualification.

At the truck, source-grounded Parker 2202P-6-6 street elbows turn the open C5.6 coupling ports upward and backward. Straight adapter/swivel chains would have aimed the hoses into the existing centre crossbeam. Each elbow is followed by the retained 017M/17B25 fitting family and a 70 mm exposed straight lead. A central 12 mm gusset supports the short twin-guide shelf. Its root contacts the back face of the existing C5.6 AIR-ANCHOR-PLATE at local q=0, v=−10 to +20 mm; no new hole or reshaping of that plate is made. The gusset bends around the anchor-plate upper edge and supports the back of the guide shelf, avoiding the guide bores. Nominal fitting thread engagements remain scenarios.

The relocated local air origin is (270, 0, 450) mm. The extended AIR-SUPPORT-BASE occupies X160–296.2 mm, with its original supported span ending at X246.2 mm. Its three original beam attachment welds stay in place on the unchanged HSS. No weld is invented beneath the 50 mm plate overhang. Air-support bending, fatigue and weld capacity remain open. The local chamber endpoints stay fixed to the same moving assembly; only their truck-side controls and support points shift.

The new free hoses run from truck endpoints (194.5516, −150/−215, 569.0933) mm to body endpoints (390, −150/−215, 740) mm. Nominal cubic handles are 160 and 90 mm. Each nominal free portion measures 321.0109 mm; each end also has a separately modeled 70 mm straight lead. These are exposed geometric lengths, not hose cutting instructions. Manufacturer crimp data, insertion, free-length requirements and pressure growth remain unresolved.

A three-millimetre stand-off guard with six-millimetre corner radii sits between the body bolster and the free hoses. It attaches to the body backing plate with actual welded tabs. Hose contact with the guard is not intended or credited as acceptable; the motion review must keep it clear.

Kinematic boundary

The model is expressed in the nominal C5 truck frame. The body rotates about the actual spherical centre (0, 0, 830.7) mm. The independent check uses yaw relative to the truck and a two-degree resultant tilt cone. Primary axle travel moves the axle, brake chamber and existing local hoses relative to the truck; it must not be added again as body-to-truck anchor heave.

The body and truck fitting axes are fixed to their respective mounts. A 17B25 installation swivel is not a continuously rotating hose joint. A collision-free constant-length centreline does not establish acceptable hose twist, pressure flex, fatigue, abrasion or life. Those are separate manufacturer/prototype holds. Physical port labels PORT97 and PORT116 remain unassigned until ZF confirms the service and spring-release identities.

Source and preservation

The builder imports the frozen C5.6 BREP and duplicates existing fitting/guide dimensional envelopes with declared rigid transforms. Source-controlled envelope dimensions are distinguished from unresolved local profiles. Only M, N and D are sourced for the new 2202P elbow; its outer extrusion profile, thread outline and counterbore details are provisional. The detailed hardware record is research-air-hardware.json.

The checkpoint contains 279 valid parts. The 199 parent parts are partitioned into 156 unchanged shapes, 40 rigidly relocated air parts and three revised shapes: the baseplate and two local free hoses. There are 80 additions. Of the 156 unchanged shapes, 154 retain identical BREP serialization. The two unchanged chamber-fitting shapes have different serialization after import/export sharing, but zero Boolean symmetric difference and matching volumes/bounds. interface-geometry-preservation.json records this explicitly. The 105 selected primary mounting parts remain in the unchanged category.

Intended contact pairs in the geometry contract identify actual bracket/weld, bulkhead, guide and hose-end connections for independent review. A listed pair is an expected interface, not a substitute for testing its final shape. All engineering conclusions must bind the final checkpoint hash after contact and movement review.

Motion review

C5.7 body-to-truck hose movement review

Discrete body-to-truck hose and support geometry screening. No fabrication or operating approval.

45 body postures × 32 primary/wear postures = 1440 combined configurations. 90 supply curves and 64 local curves were regenerated with constant geometric free length. Supply minimum bend radius: 51.828 mm; local minimum: 66.676 mm. Catalog nominal comparison: 45 mm, without claiming dynamic-duty approval. Supply-to-supply free-curve clearance lower bound: 44.205 mm. Maximum length residual: 1e-09 mm.

Maximum supply end-clock mismatch change after rotation-minimizing transport: 21.084 degrees. This is an unresolved geometric torsion-compatibility diagnostic, not a permitted hose twist or an elastic stress calculation. The fittings are not credited as live swivels.

Positive intersections: nominal new hardware 0, rigid movement 0, supply hose 0, revised local hose/all hardware 0, supply/local hose 0, local/local hose 0. Calculation errors: 0.

Connection review

227 nominal contacts; 0 new rigid parts lack a contact path to retained geometry. These are exact CAD-contact observations, not joint-strength findings.

Shared-pose model

yaw+7-bodyroll+2-axleroll+2-jounce-wear25.4. All stated components share the same body posture, primary travel and wear offset. The JSON stores the exact transforms and regenerated curves.

Limits

  • The 45 body postures combine five yaw values with zero tilt or a 2 degree resultant tilt cone at eight azimuths; independent 2 degree roll plus 2 degree pitch is not used.
  • All 32 primary suspension/wear states are combined wherever relative geometry requires it, yielding 1440 distinct body/primary configurations. These are discrete configurations, not a continuous sweep.
  • Body and truck move together in primary heave. Supply endpoints articulate only through the declared body-to-truck yaw/tilt transform. Primary travel affects clearance to the axle, chamber, link, and preserved local hoses.
  • Constant-length Bezier curves preserve endpoint tangents but do not solve material twist, pressure growth, clamp friction, strain, whipping, fatigue, drainage, or actual installed-hose equilibrium.
  • The Bishop-frame diagnostic measures the change in rigid-end clock mismatch after rotation-minimizing transport along the geometric centerline. Any nonzero result is unresolved torsional compatibility; no allowable twist or live-swivel rotation is credited.
  • Geometric free lengths and straight leads are not production cut lengths. Crimp insertion, actual interfaces, tolerances, slack allowance and supplier-approved rail dynamic duty remain open.
  • A positive modeled clearance is not a tolerance or maintenance allowance. Purchased external profiles, elbow/fitting engagement, port assignment, valve/control topology and upstream coach plumbing remain provisional.
  • The relocated C56 local air hardware and regenerated local hoses are checked anew against all relevant current fixed, primary-moving and body targets. No previous local-hose clearance result is used to cover the revised geometry.
  • C5 body-context/upper-joint geometry and the isolated C54 brake axle remain the coordinate reference. Separate CW3 wheelset study and whole-coach flexible pipes are not substituted or accepted by this local audit.
  • Opposite axle and spring-coil deformation are not independently re-solved. Existing C56 structural force, fit, preload, fatigue and supplier holds remain open. No brake station is duplicated.

Structural status review

C5.7 structural status

The C56 brake carrier, keyed hub, bearing arrangement, torque link, retained pins and frame brake mounting are unchanged. The independent frozen-geometry comparison verifies all 105 parts supporting the retained C56-D01/D02/D03 sections have identical BREP serializations. The local air assembly moved 50 mm along +X, its base was extended over its unchanged HSS attachment, and its two free hose curves were regenerated. These air-support changes do not revise the brake torque path.

Both existing supplier threads and a broader inbound mail search were checked for this issue. No reply or new controlled drawing was found. Rotor interface discrepancies, exact caliper/pad/chamber matching, physical port assignment and maximum actuator forces therefore remain unresolved. A nominal pressure-area calculation is not substituted for guaranteed maximum service, parking or compounded output.

Earlier bearing, key, hub, pin, weld and frame calculations remain development screens. Their load, material, fit, fatigue and preload limitations are not closed by the hose layout. Final sizing and other brake locations remain pending the requested supplier data.

The revised local support and new body/truck air brackets carry proposed hose/end-fitting reactions only. Their welds, plate thicknesses, engagements and contact paths describe physical geometry; installation loads, local bolster/HSS effects, fatigue, tolerances and field validation remain open. No structural, fabrication or operating release is assigned.

Research service access

C5.6 service-access research

The service drawings can show the sourced chamber axis, 43 mm studs and the 89 mm rear release projection, together with actual retained custom metal. They cannot supply missing pad shapes, caliper cavities or tool datums.

The PAN17 sequence keeps the chamber installed for pad work. After gaining wheel-side access, it removes the retainer and three springs, retracts the adjuster, slides the caliper toward the wheel for the outer pad, then toward the chamber for the inner pad and pressure plate. The road-vehicle wheel-removal step is not automatically a usable railway procedure. Published AF interfaces are 8 mm adjuster, 17 mm retainer, 24 mm chamber nut and 14 mm guide-pin socket. Use a ring spanner on the adjuster. The illustrations are generic family examples. [WABCO manual, printed pp13-17,33-38]

The chamber GA fixes its rear body at s=240.5 mm and released screw tip at s=329.5 mm from the chamber mounting face. Its 89 mm extension does not include hand or wrench clearance. Existing Ø20 extension and Ø60 x50 tool solids are explicit design reservations; their diameters are not OEM requirements. The Rmax110 stored-release-fixture bound still needs its actual profile and orientation. The global chamber mounting datum remains provisional. [WABCO9253800230 GA, exact0250 row]

A real socket candidate is Stahlwille52-24, article03030024: 24 mm AF, 42 mm length, maximum32 mm OD, 26 mm drive-end OD, 21 mm intermediate bore, and half-inch drive. A full32 mm diameter envelope is conservative for external reach screening. It does not prove internal stud/nut engagement or torque-wrench access. The current nut positions are illustrative, so every installed result retains that dependency. [Stahlwille catalog]

An 8 mm adjuster-tool space candidate is Stahlwille20 8x10, article41040810:180 mm long,13.1/15.8 mm ring ODs,7/9 mm ring thicknesses,21 mm offset,75-degree crank. Exact adjustment-tool equivalence and positioning remain unapproved. [Stahlwille catalog]

Chamber-removal views must disconnect hoses and remove fastening nuts before translating the chamber. TRISTOP instructions prohibit adding washers or reinforcement plates at its attachment and require specified replacement nuts. None of this closes purchased-component matching or structural loads.

Rotor and caliper package evidence

C5.5 rotor and PAN17 package evidence

Keep Webb 55030PK-65 as a conditional development envelope. Do not release its custom hub or permit automatic substitution with S-33391, CT826 or a 173 mm pilot rotor. The public evidence establishes a replaceable product family and supply routes, but does not yet establish the exact purchased rotor/pad/hub mating section.

What changed in this revision

The disagreement is now supported by primary manufacturers. Webb's current R32 flyer gives a 6.65 inch pilot and 7.71 inch PCD. Its 2022 catalog gives the same pilot but a 7.72 inch PCD. Haldex, DuraBrake and Meritor publish approximately 173 mm pilots for their cross-referenced CT826 replacements. Those figures must not be blended into a single machining specification.

Exact productODNew / discard thicknessPilotPCDOverall depthMounting

Meritor M44D75180325 mm34 / 28 mm173 mm196 mm149 mm8 × M14 × 2

Hendrickson S-33391325 mmFull controlled section unavailableUnresolvedUnresolvedUnresolvedOEM kit contains 8 bolts

Webb figures are inch-table conversions, not tolerances. *The 1.11 inch discard value is reported by the official product-page search index and must be checked against the actual rotor marking. The direct page currently exposes JavaScript placeholders.

Meritor's own PB1461 catalog adds a 13 mm flange thickness and a generic sectional datum. Its nominal flange-face to disc-centre distance would be 149 − 34/2 = 132 mm. That is a catalog-section interpretation, not an individual controlled drawing. Its annulus, precise hat profile, thread depth and mating tolerances remain absent. Switching to it would not close the present fit requirement.

This rejects an unproved interchange for our custom hub. It does not claim that manufacturer-approved replacements cannot work on their intended original hubs.

Caliper and pad evidence

The legacy Hendrickson catalog pairs S-33391 with PAN17 calipers S-39094-L/R (WABCO 40175072/73), S-41159-1 / WABCO 232 pads and the legacy 20/24 chamber family. This confirms an OEM application package. It does not confirm the Webb substitution or custom mount.

WABCO's exact 40175072 BOM independently names pad kit 12999806. Its product page provides no pad-outline drawing. Do not equate this kit with S-41159-1, or use shipping-box dimensions as pad dimensions.

The controlled WABCO GA is useful for the caliper interface:

  • Reference disc: 330 mm diameter, new thickness 34 mm with −0.2 / +0 mm tolerance, wear limit 28 mm.
  • Disc installation datum: 24 ± 1 mm from the carrier mounting face to the nearer friction face. This does not locate the rotor relative to its hub.
  • Pad assembly dimension: 34.7 mm with +0.4 / −0 mm tolerance. Approximate rubbing width is 76 mm; this is not the exact selected pad outline or Webb annulus.
  • Carrier attachment: six oiled M14 × 1.5 grade 10.9 bolts, at least 18 mm thread engagement, 205–225 N·m tightening torque, numbered order 1–6.
  • Maximum transferable connection torque: 10,000 N·m. This is not the actual brake output or a torque cap for combined service and spring force.

No controlled carrier tapped depth or bolt-hole entry chamfer was found. An M14 × 30 bolt through a 12 mm plate provides only 18 mm nominal penetration before tolerances and incomplete threads. Full thread engagement must still be demonstrated.

No source established exact selected-pad swept radii, permissible overhang on the 325.12 mm rotor, or the hot and worn clearance. The Gigant PAN17-1 report contains useful force and annulus data for a different 335 mm / Jurid 539 / Type 24 package; those data are excluded from this design's calculations.

Chamber evidence and alternatives

C55 exact chamber research

Decision: retain WABCO 9253800250/9253800240 as the legacy PAN17/WABCO 232/20-24 engineering reference. This is a hold, not a purchased-package or fabrication release. Research date:2026-10-20. This original research preceded dispatch. The Webb and ZF requests have since been sent; no purchase was made.

ZF now publicly lists 9253800460 as the open-hole alternative to 0250, and 9253800450 to 0240. This is an exact manufacturer relationship, recovered from the public product page’s extendedModel.extProdData.AlternativeProductsInfo; it is labelled an alternative, not a formal supersession. The new family drawing explicitly includes 0460/0450. It does not provide the missing guaranteed maximum force data or establish readily available stock of the exact pair. Therefore it is a credible replacement candidate, not a reason to silently substitute another chamber in C55.

ItemControlled or verified evidenceRemaining limit
Service/spring pressureBoth GAs specify 8 bar working and 10 bar temporaryTemporary duty/duration and maximum transmitted force unknown
Service outputInformational curves at 1.38,2.76,4.14,5.52 and 6.59 barNo guaranteed maximum at 8 or 10 bar; no extrapolation used
Parking outputLegacy GA has informational spring plotsNo certified full-stroke maximum/tolerance bound; new GA has no parking plot
CompoundingZF iEBS manual§4.2.6 describes a system function preventing additive chamber forcesCoach circuit and fault behavior unselected; intrinsic chamber protection not established
Mass0250 catalog 7.0 kg;0460 catalog 7.2 kgExact CG/inertia and supported assembly CG not supplied
Mount120.7±0.3 mm stud pitch, M16×1.5,43 mm projection,15±0.5 mm R8 pushrodExact caliper mating centre, torque-policy resolution and tolerance stack remain open
Ports3/8-18NPTF,97/116 mm axial stations,89 mm above axis and±25 mm lateral offsets in main viewWhich real boss is cast 11/12, tap depth and fitting insertion remain open
Caging89 mm rear projection drawnFull removal/wrench/hand envelope not controlled

The main 0460/0450 envelope matches the published legacy dimensions:240.5 mm mount-face-to-body-end,Ø184 maximum circular body,Ø158.5 rear shell,Ø153 front,Ø54×4.1 pilot andR110 stored-hardware bound. The selected 0460 row has 300°port clocking;0450 has 60°; both clamp angles are 270°. The new rear shell has fourØ6 breather holes and an explicitly labelled 1/2ACMErelease mechanism. These details and drainage attitude still require exact-variant verification before substitution. Catalog package-size fields were not used as body dimensions.

The 89 mm port-face dimension supports C55’s correction of the earlier 88 mm placement. It does not close guessed boss topology or service/spring hose identification. The caliper chamber-axis radial coordinate 191.5 mm and 9°inclination are dimensioned; the existing axialq 134.011 mm placement remains a provisional interpretation of the 129.6 mm endpoint, not a controlled OEM datum.

The iEBS anti-compounding function feeds release air into the spring chamber during service application. It belongs to the brake control system. An internal breather valve does not prove anti-compounding. Simultaneous and fault loads therefore remain in the unresolved structural load envelope. Nominal 20-square-inch chamber area, another PAN17 variant’s ratio, family torque ratings and generic spring warnings were not used as substitute maxima.

Remaining force and strength gaps

C55 first-brake strength-gap review

No metal change or mounted-brake rating is justified yet. The exact maximum service and parking outputs remain unconfirmed, and simultaneous service/spring action has not been excluded by a selected anti-compounding circuit. The controlled WABCO 40175072 GA, callout 1, limits the specified carrier connection to 10 kNm; this is not an actuator-output or combined-force cap. The final input envelope must remain within that connection limit. Reinforcing custom metal cannot raise the OEM limit.

The largest reported nominal metal stress is at the 100 mm axle/hub key contact; 30 mm pin bending and the web-to-barrel weld also need material-based limits. Their actual capacity ranking is unresolved because the relevant allowables are unspecified. The largest unresolved mechanism is the split hub/shaft load path; the mounted bearings also need low-load, fit and thermal-clearance disposition before their catalog ratings can be used.

Load-dependent screens

With T in kNm per disc, the existing pose grid gives the following upper screens. They include the separate 50 kg gravity probe at its assumed CG; actual shock, actual mass/CG and any smaller pad radius require new calculation.

ItemDemand expression10 kNm reference
Outer radial bearing15.54491T + 0.68158 kN156.13 kN actual grid
Pin combined stress22.43944T + 1.10013 MPa225.49 MPa actual grid
Key projected bearing34.7222T MPa347.22 MPa
Key shear9.92063T MPa99.21 MPa
Web/barrel candidate 6 mm fillet14.9024T MPa, braking resultant only149.02 MPa

There is no approved allowable for the key, pin, custom plates, hub or welds. The key allowable must cover the weakest of shaft, key and hub contact material; a harder key alone does not qualify the other two. The rotor-bolt and split-joint load paths need separate equilibrium and preload/contact calculations.

Catalog-based torque thresholds only

SKF static-factor guidance varies with load certainty and tolerated permanent deformation. For continuous motion with low load certainty, its table gives progressively stricter factors 2.5, 3 and 4. Those factors produce these conditional radial-bearing thresholds using 255 kN C0 and the bound above:

Static factorConditional torque ceiling
2.56.518 kNm
35.424 kNm
44.057 kNm

These are bearing static checks, not system ratings or an adopted operating pressure. Catalog dynamic C=99 kN is not an allowable force. Bearing life requires the rotational duty/load spectrum, lubrication, contamination and temperature; SKF basic roller life uses (C/P)^(10/3). [SKF static guidance; SKF life]

SKF also estimates minimum loads of 1.98 kN radial and 0.340 kN axial for these selected bearings. The current 50 kg coasting probe gives outer radial no more than 0.682 kN and nominal axial zero. Obtain supplier disposition for low-load coasting and the actual speed/grease before retaining the arrangement. Do not cure this by an uncalculated hard preload. [SKF minimum load]

Correct the screw screen before using it

The exact M14×1.5 fine thread has catalog tensile area 125 mm²; C54 used the 115 mm² coarse-thread value. If threads cross the plate/caliper shear plane, a plain 14 mm shank area is also inappropriate. Basic external-root geometry gives 116.13 mm²; the actual screw tolerance and thread root still need its drawing. [M14 fine thread]

Disc torquePlain-shank shearBasic thread-root shearAxial external-load stress using 125 mm²
1 kNm10.28 MPa13.63 MPa3.67 MPa
3 kNm30.84 MPa40.88 MPa11.01 MPa
6 kNm61.68 MPa81.77 MPa22.02 MPa
10 kNm102.80 MPa136.28 MPa36.69 MPa

These are external-load stresses, not preload or bolt-connection acceptance. The controlled GA specifies Albion NJJ4163 screws with 18 mm wrench size, M14×1.5 grade 10.9, oiled; it requires 205–225 Nm tightening in the specified order and 18 mm minimum engagement. A 30 mm screw through 12 mm plate supplies only 18 mm nominal penetration; the GA does not establish the available full-thread depth or entry chamfer. Verify the worst-case stack and bottoming before selecting length. The two smallest crescent edge ligaments are only 6.578 mm; actual plate prying/bearing/breakout analysis must replace arbitrary strip probes. [WABCO carrier interface]

Specific closure work

ConnectionWhat must close
Keyed axle and split hubSpecify shaft/key/hub material condition and keyway roots. Solve both hub halves, split-screw preload/contact and rotor attachment. Recheck rotating axle bending/fatigue under wheel loads and brake-bearing reactions; pure torsion is insufficient.
Needle-bearing seatsSpecify 100/120/150 mm fits, split closure, seat roundness/stiffness and post-weld machining. Verify residual internal clearance at hot/cold limits and supplier low-load suitability.
Opposed thrust stacksReplace zero nominal axial clearance with a controlled measured end-float/preload design. The 175 mm path changes 0.21 mm for an illustrative 100 K steel differential; this is not a proposed tolerance. Include thermal force when checking covers, M4/M6 retention and collars.
Carrier and frame weldsEstablish actual accessible bead paths, WPS/base metals/filler and fatigue detail. Analyze supporting barrel wall, 12 mm side webs and local plates with existing truck loads. Add caliper/chamber gravity and shock at real CG. Full 19,348.8 mm² endplate backing proves contact only.
30 mm pins and retainersEstablish material/heat treatment, contact/clearance and bending fatigue. The 45 mm support-span model is bending-controlled. Select and verify actual cotter/washer retention.

The exact 9253800250 chamber catalog mass is 7.0 kg, but its controlled CG is unavailable. A shell-envelope centroid cannot substitute for it. Fit selection must account for ring expansion, seat discontinuity, temperature and remaining bearing clearance. [SKF fits]

Concrete bearing-mount simplification to assess

A one-piece OD 120 / ID 100 mm sleeve still cannot pass the retained Ø110 mm wheel seat after wheel removal. That axle shoulder is why a split reducer is needed.

A continuous OD 120 / ID 110.5 mm outer sleeve over a uniform split Ø100→110.5 mm inner adapter could give continuous bearing/seal seats and avoid the five stepped assembly stages. Its outer wall is only 4.75 mm: sleeve distortion, both interface fits, creep, axial backing and thermal clearance need analysis before adoption. A revised axle with integral Ø120 mm bearing/seal seats would eliminate both reducer interfaces, but requires a new axle drawing, transitions and fatigue review. Neither concept is a released geometry change.

Completion sequence

1. Freeze the exact package and upper service, parking and combined/fault loads; use lower friction/force separately for required stopping and grade holding. Include pressure protection, real mass/CG and duty. 2. Resolve bearing minimum-load/seat/axial-clearance questions and specify materials, fasteners and weld criteria. 3. Recalculate all local connections and axle fatigue at those bounds; change only parts that fail an explicit criterion. 4. Build one reviewed development installation and verify assembly, cold/hot drag/end float, runout, retention and mount deflection at an agreed proof load. Complete the defined thermal/cyclic test and inspection; a static proof does not demonstrate fatigue life. 5. Repeat the installation only after that evidence closes.

Remaining geometry and service gaps

C5.5 geometry gap review

C5.5 changes the air interfaces of one installation. It preserves C5.4 custom metal, the revised axle keyway, bearing stack, hub, torque link and frame support. It does not establish a commercially qualified brake package.

Four inherited envelope intersections

C5.4 pairVolume, mm³Cause and C5.5 treatment
Chamber intermediate envelope / fitting at station 97983.002An assumed 8 mm male-thread insertion intersects an unbored chamber cylinder. Separate the unresolved thread-interface reservation from the external fitting. Do not invent a port cavity.
Chamber intermediate envelope / fitting at station 116983.056Same unresolved mating interface. Exact boss section, engagement and thread sealing/clocking are still required.
Fitting / hose at station 9727.708The swept profile used a sampled chord, tilted 3.261° from the port axis. Exact Bezier geometry with the analytic tangent removes this modeling artifact.
Fitting / hose at station 11627.743Same artifact, with 3.265° tilt. Scratch exact-solid tests produced zero intersection for both corrected mouths.

The first two findings were not evidence of two known pieces of metal occupying the same space. Separating the reservations does not prove the real threaded joints fit. The last two were reproducible CAD construction errors.

Source corrections and component boundaries

The controlled WABCO 9253800230 GA row for 9253800250 gives the port-face construction as 89 mm radial ordinate, ±25 mm tangential offset, at 97/116 mm behind the mounting face. C5.4 used an unsupported 88 mm radial ordinate. C5.5 corrects it by 1 mm along the actual port normal. The 300° clocking is retained.

The PAN17 manual defines cast 11 as service and 12 as spring/parking. Neither the available GA nor the manual establishes the cast-number assignment to the 97/116 mm stations. C5.5 therefore labels geometry by station, not by an unverified air-circuit function.

The fitting remains Parker 10125-6-6B-VS: published overall length 44 mm and AF 17.4625 mm. Exact crimp shell, hex lengths and installed NPTF engagement are unavailable. The inherited 8 mm insertion is retained only as an explicit placement scenario. Hose 271-6 has nominal OD 19 mm and catalogue minimum bend radius 45 mm. Its new CAD is an annular, exact cubic sweep.

Moving hoses

A fixed remote endpoint and tangent plus the transformed chamber endpoint/tangent define a candidate constant-length family. Keep the initial handle at 150 mm; solve the final handle for the same integrated curve length. The C5.4 pilot calculation covered 10 suspension poses and both wear limits: minimum radii were 79.0 and 83.8 mm. Simply dragging the first two control points changed length by about −24 to +19 mm and was rejected.

C5.5 must recompute those results after the datum correction, then check both hoses against each other, custom parts and the complete retained C5 truck/body. Sampling is a diagnostic, not a physical hose acceptance. Actual fixed couplings, clamp lead lengths, torsion limits, rub clearance, manufacturing allowance and flex validation remain missing.

Service access

The PAN17 pad procedure retains the chamber, removes the retainer and three springs, retracts adjustment, slides the caliper in both directions, and removes pads/pressure plate. Its vehicle procedure removes the road wheel. The railway installation cannot inherit an on-car service claim from this manual. C5.4's single generic pad-withdrawal box is removed because it does not represent that sequence.

Tool interfaces are AF 8 mm at the adjuster, AF 17 mm at the lining retainer bolt and AF 24 mm at the chamber nuts. These values do not define wrench swings or socket/hand envelopes. Chamber removal requires isolated air, mechanical spring release, disconnected hoses and mounting-nut removal. A 43 mm axial translation only clears the published stud projection from an ideal mounting plane; actual caliper seats, nuts, tools and the complete removal route remain open.

Final moving-hose geometry screen

C5.5 moving-hose geometry review

Bounded constant-length moving-hose geometry audit. No fabrication or operation approval.

Evaluated 40 constant-length hose curves. Minimum calculated radius: 78.959 mm against 45 mm nominal. Maximum arc-length residual: 8.66 e-11 mm. Mutual hose clearance lower bound: 13.114 mm using adaptive Bezier/chord bounds. Exact solid tests: 82; positive intersections: 0; calculation errors: 0. Maximum relative end-frame rotation: 2.1033 deg rees. This is not a hose torsion acceptance.

The fixed-end connection, end straight lengths and physical hose properties remain provisional. These curves demonstrate a bounded geometric candidate, not the shape a pressure-loaded hose is guaranteed to adopt.

Remaining limits

  • Geometric, constant-length candidate curves only. No constitutive bending, pressure growth, fatigue, dynamic whipping or material torsion model.
  • Remote endpoints/tangents are the current provisional truck-mounted contract. Actual bulkhead clamps, hose cut length, insertion and required straight leads must be confirmed.
  • Relative end-frame rotation is not a permissible torsional strain or a twist pass. Required orientation, permitted hose twist and physical flex validation remain open.
  • Tube radius 9.5 mm and curvature limit 45 mm are nominal catalogue dimensions; no manufacturing, heat, abrasion or deflection allowance is included.
  • Ten suspension/maintenance cases at two wear values, plus three yaw and three body-roll values, are discrete samples rather than a continuous configuration-space certification.
  • Bezier hull subdivision conservatively encloses each idealized swept hose. Close candidates use actual annular circular-profile sweeps and exact BREP intersection tests.
  • Opposite axle and coil shapes remain at nominal geometry. No complete train air circuit or remaining brake locations are qualified.
  • Unknown caliper/rotor/port/fitting material outlines remain supplier envelopes. Their contacts cannot be dismissed or declared physical without controlled geometry.
  • No fabrication, installation or operation approval is issued.

The complete purchased-parts fit remains conditional.

The documented chamber outline replaces the former generic reservation. Its drawing specifies 8 bar working pressure and 10 bar temporary maximum. The information-only force curves do not establish the exact maximum actuator load at those limits. PAN17's 10 kNm family reference is used as a comparison, not a certified delivered-torque ceiling. The exact legacy chamber's stock and lead time are unconfirmed. Current 22/24 / 238-pad references are a separate package and are not substituted into this geometry.

Installation scope

Only one right-side brake is issued. The CAD shows the revised radial and thrust support, axle attachment, articulated torque link and frame connection. The truck viewer uses the current keyed axle and removes superseded outboard brake parts from all four locations; the other three locations remain unfinished. Service reservations and local hose geometry are development definitions, not a completed full-train air circuit or proof of safe access. C54 structural development screen

DEVELOPMENT SCREEN, NOT FABRICATION RELEASE

The revised carrier uses two SKF NA 4824 radial needle bearings, separate opposed AXK thrust assemblies and Ruland split shaft collars. A 200 mm torque arm acts through one spherical-ended link. The frame crossmember is HSS 6 × 4 × 1/2 inch.

10,000 Nm is a comparison load. Actual maximum actuator torque is not yet established. These figures are not allowable loads or fabrication approval.

Actual connections and local demand

The final caliper mount plate is 12.0 mm thick. At the 10 kNm reference, its six-bolt elastic group has maximum shear 15.825 kN and maximum axial bolt load 4.587 kN. The complete force and moment balances are checked. Bolt, plate, thread, prying and fatigue capacities are not approved.

The largest calculated axial carrier reaction is 2.162 kN. It acts through the opposed SKF thrust cages and covers to a Ruland collar. The manufacturer publishes 88.95 kN axial load for MSPH-100-F under its specified shaft and installation conditions; application, contamination and temperature checks remain open.

The direct split rotor hub uses one nominal 100 mm key. With 72 mm conservative effective flank length, the 10 kNm reference gives 99.21 MPa key shear and 347.22 MPa projected key bearing. These are demands, not qualified material capacities. The split-joint screws, positive shoulder stop, rotor attachment and keyed axle fatigue remain unresolved.

The single installed brake has unequal end reactions. For the ideal uniform beam with both ends restrained in torsion, the right support carries the larger share. A sensitivity also assigns all torsion to one end. The symmetric two-brake comparison used for the gross frame and end-weld screens envelopes these nominal one-brake bending and torsion demands; actual end stiffness still requires qualification.

The full motion-grid end-weld demands, using a candidate 6 mm fillet, reach 89.19 MPa at the HSS-to-endplate perimeter and 73.86 MPa at the endplate-to-side-web perimeter. These are complete load-transfer demands without an approved weld allowable.

The thrust-cover OD is 172 mm. At the six M4 screw locations, the nominal countersink-to-outer-edge ligament is 2.75 mm; each screw has 360.4 N nominal equal-share tension under the reference axial reaction. Cover bending, thread stripping, preload and locking remain unqualified.

Remaining limits

  • Public chamber curves are not a verified caliper pressure-to-torque transfer. Actual maximum brake torque remains unknown.
  • 0/50 kg stationary mass at local [100,mount flange Y,0] is a sensitivity, not a verified complete-purchased-assembly mass or conservative upper bound.
  • Needle bearings carry radial load only. The separate opposed AXK stacks carry axial reaction; their support plates, fit and end float require verification.
  • C0 is a catalogue reference, not an allowable load. No shock factor, safety factor, combined duty spectrum, fatigue life or thermal qualification is established.
  • Gross frame calculation assumes symmetric same-axle two-brake forces and equally shared end torsion. Local bracket, weld, endplate and side-web distortion remain unqualified.
  • Pin bending uses a conservative central point load over actual cheek-centroid spacing. Material, cross-hole, seat pressure, fatigue and retention qualification remain open.
  • The former two-bearing force solver was audited against a separately assembled 6x6 force/moment system. Axial reaction is now assigned to the thrust stack explicitly.
  • Equilibrium does not prove clearance, pad/rotor compatibility, hose routing or service access. See independent motion/contact records.

The review prompted correction of the mirrored 0250 port clocking, HSS/pivot interference, articulated pin-spacer/retainer contact and tight nominal hose bends. The stepped bearing reducer was segmented to address trapped seal tracks. Its complete assembly sequence remains open.

The represented chamber is the exact legacy 9253800250 candidate with source-based two-stud pattern, 15 mm pushrod projection, 240.5 mm body length and 89 mm caging extension. Mating-coordinate uncertainty, idealized contours and missing stored-release hardware prevent a complete fit claim. The exact GA permits 8 bar working pressure and 10 bar temporarily. Its informational force graph does not certify maximum output force.

Bounded findings

  • V01 154/154 checkpoint shapes are valid. The geometry and checker hashes remained unchanged during the run.
  • V02 317 nominal exact pair tests found zero unresolved custom-material overlaps. The 40 positives comprise 36 explicit screw-major-cylinder/tap-drill representations and four purchased fitting/chamber/hose envelope junctions.
  • V03 The relocated HSS clears the actual retained pivot cup. Tube top Z 400.8 is 13.9 mm below cup minimum Z 414.7; this nominal separation is not a tolerance allowance.
  • V04 Both endplates share 19,348.8 mm² with the actual C5 inner side webs, the full 166.8×116 mm rectangle. This establishes backing, not weld strength or accessible weld length.
  • V05 Ten finite-link poses close within 5.7×10⁻¹⁴ mm: nine regular journal-admissible poses and a separate zero-roll −60 mm captured maintenance pose. 1239 exact moving-pair tests found no custom-part interference.
  • V06 The represented rigid parts had no positive intersections with 276 retained fixed C5 shapes or 82 body shapes in the sampled yaw, body-roll and wear cases. Checks against 63 axle 2 shapes found only 10 new-key/uncut-axle witness intersections.
  • V07 The corrected 0250 port vector is 300° clockwise from the upper stud when looking into the mounting face. The earlier mirrored vector corresponded to the 60° variant.
  • V08 The source nominal chamber/caliper interfaces agree at 120.7 mm stud pitch and M16×1.5. The model has two studs and supplied-nut references without intervening washers. Installed grip and nut access remain open.
  • V09 Independent 2001-point nominal cubic checks give minimum hose radii 97.165/96.499 mm, above the recorded 45 mm minimum. Nominal hose exteriors have no retained-C5 or mutual hose intersection. Hose movement is unqualified.
  • V10 The shoulder spacer, three 100 mm collars and direct rotor hub are split. The reducer is now five axial sections of two halves, with separate 110.5 mm seal-support sections. This addresses the specific monolithic stepped-reducer trap; the staged assembly and fits remain open.

Open items

  • H01 Exact rotor/caliper/pad/chamber compatibility, 325.12 mm rotor versus 330 mm caliper GA, certified maximum service/spring forces and exact replacement-part availability remain upstream gates. Informational force curves do not certify a maximum force.
  • H02 Chamber mating coordinate q 134.011 mm is interpreted, not controlled. It follows 129.6+28.2 sin 9° only if the identified endpoint is the upper opening edge. Exact caliper material geometry and the rotor mating-face datum remain missing.
  • H03 Chamber shell transitions are idealized. The source Rmax 110 stored release-fixture/clamp protrusion exceeds the circular body and is not fully represented. Port bosses, breather locations and complete stored/released hardware need controlled CAD or the actual part.
  • H04 The 43 mm stud projection is sourced, but actual caliper grip, nut seat, thread runout/protrusion and wrench approach are not established. Use supplied nuts without added washers or locking aids; supplier torque/flatness instructions govern.
  • H05 SERVICE and SPRING labels at 97/116 mm stations are intended allocations only. Verify cast port identifiers and exact supplier hand code before connecting either circuit.
  • H06 Flexible hose length, twist, end constraints, full 25.4 mm wear, suspension travel, captured droop, abrasion, clips and minimum bend through motion remain unqualified. The supply ends are provisional.
  • H07 The 89 mm rear caging projection is sourced, but the added 50 mm tool zone is arbitrary. Clear reservations do not prove tool insertion, turning arc, hand access or the complete OEM release procedure. Nut-removal access is also open.
  • H08 The pad-withdrawal box is provisional. Actual pads, retaining hardware, caliper movement and tools must establish the removal sequence.
  • H09 After wheel removal, 120 mm bearing bores and 110.5 mm seal-ring bores can nominally pass the 110 mm wheel seat; the latter has only 0.5 mm diametral margin. Final tolerances, chamfers, bearing fits, segmented-reducer staging, continuous ground seal tracks and axial end clearance are unqualified.
  • H10 Rotor replacement requires wheelset removal and shop removal of one wheel. The closed rotor cannot pass a 600 mm wheel in place. Pressing/removal tools, surface protection, reassembly inspections and the new axle keyway specification remain unfinished.
  • H11 These discrete samples are not a continuous sweep or tolerance stack. Full rotating hardware, Ruland screw pockets, real spherical-bearing contours, thermal/elastic effects and all manufacturing tolerances remain open. Caliper and rotor-hat projections were excluded from material acceptance.
  • H12 Load, fatigue and weld qualification belongs to the separate structural report. Other brake locations, handed assemblies and the complete reservoir/relay/control/parking air circuit are outside this review.

Assembly direction to develop

Remove the wheelset and one axlebox/wheel using a qualified shop procedure. Pass closed bearings, housing and continuous seal tracks over the nominal 110 mm wheel seat before installing 100 mm split collars or split hub sections. Stage separate 110.5 mm seal-support pieces and closed tracks before closing the neighboring 120 mm reducer sections. Establish that the bearing/housing module can then be seated without damaging ground tracks or transmitting installation load through rolling elements. Determine fits, pressing/heating method, axial retention and end clearance before treating this as an assembly procedure. The topology suggests a route; no physical or animated assembly demonstration has been completed.

The JSON retains every positive pair, pose, source hash and exclusion. Caliper/rotor projections, unknown stored chamber hardware, hose deformation, tolerance and fatigue remain outside an accepted fit result.

Historical C5.3 issue. C5.4 above is the current brake installation record.

C5.3 · R0 · 19 OCTOBER 2026

Inboard disc brakes with an available replacement rotor

Inboard disc-and-caliper development around the separately replaceable Webb 55030PK-65 rotor. Custom hub, axle-following caliper carrier and short articulated frame reaction are shown in CAD. The exact rotor/caliper contact, chamber envelope and maximum output remain unresolved; this is not a fabrication release.

Open the dimensioned drawings and CAD.

Replacement part and mounting basis

Webb 55030PK-65 is the selected rotor development basis: 325.12 mm outside diameter, 34.036 mm new friction thickness, 148.59 mm overall U-section depth, 168.91 mm pilot and eight M14 × 2 holes on a 195.834 mm circle. These conversions preserve the published inch values and do not define machining tolerances. Triple R displayed four exact rotors in stock on 19 October 2026. Stock is a dated seller observation, not reserved supply. The custom hub is tied to this exact interface; a broad PAN17 interchange listing is insufficient to substitute a different hat or pilot.

WABCO 40175072/40175073 calipers are the conditional air-disc candidates. Current Hendrickson replacement information identifies TSE 2224TLD2H-4627/4628 chambers for air service and spring parking. The rotor swept band, exact chamber drawing and maximum force remain open. This is a sourced component set under development, not a factory-matched complete kit. The smaller Wilwood hydraulic-service/cable-parking option is retained in the source comparison; it would change the braking architecture.

Location, alignment and service access

The axle-end rotor candidate intersects the retained coach girder during the yaw/jounce study. The inboard arrangement avoids that failed location. Two bearings keep the nonrotating caliper carrier aligned with the rotating axle. A short spherical-ended link transfers its torque reaction to a new truck-frame connection. The link must articulate through suspension travel without letting the carrier bind or the caliper lose alignment.

The issued CAD covers one right-side brake unit and its structural connection. Eight discs per coach are the architecture basis; the complete handed installation, opposing chambers, hose paths and service envelopes are not verified by this single module.

A one-piece inboard rotor is trapped behind the rail wheel. Major rotor replacement requires wheelset removal and shop removal/reinstallation of one wheel, followed by the controlled wheel-attachment and runout checks. A removable hub does not let the closed rotor pass over a 600 mm wheel. Routine pad and caliper access are separate service tasks; their verified tool and extraction envelopes remain part of the completion work.

C53 disc-brake mechanics — D1

Unrated development record. High torque comparison cases fail the first bearing and frame-beam screens. Maximum actuator output remains unresolved. Eight inboard discs are proposed, using axle-aligned caliper carriers and air-service/spring-parking actuation. Routine pad service is intended to remain accessible. Replacing a one-piece inboard rotor requires wheelset removal and shop rail-wheel removal/reinstallation.

The exact rotor is Webb 55030PK-65, nominally 325.12 × 34.036 mm, with a 168.91 mm pilot and eight M14 × 2 holes on a 195.834 mm pitch circle. It requires a custom hub. Hendrickson S-33391 has a different 220 mm pilot and eight M16 interface and is not assumed interchangeable. The Webb catalogue's 148.59 mm overall depth does not establish every internal web thickness or the controlled hub mating-face offset.

PAN17 calipers S-39094-L/R (WABCO 40175072/73) and S-41159-1 pads are commercial family candidates. Compatibility with this exact Webb rotor and custom hub remains conditional; this is not a matched complete commercial assembly. L949 Rev T lists 22/24 chambers S-40979-L/R / TSE 2224TLD2H-4627/4628, while its pad table retains a 20/24 reference. The exact configuration and manufacturer acceptance remain open. Earlier 18/24 or 20/24 force curves are not substituted.

Working demand and heat

Basis: 13,200 kg regular or 11,000 kg accessible coach, four axles, eight 600 mm wheels/discs, ideal equal sharing, level track / zero grade, and an assumed 1.05 rotational-inertia factor. These are working demands, not maximum component loads.

The regular 0.7 m/s² case begins at 4.066 kW per disc. Illustrative effective pad radii of 120/130/140 mm give 3.032/2.799/2.599 kN total tangential pad force. Actual torque-weighted radius must come from the installed lining contact; rotor outside radius is not a substitute. The JSON distinguishes both pads' summed normal force from one-face force. Heat input does not establish temperature, fade, cooling or repeated-stop duty. Route grade and duty remain unselected.

Output and load path

The bounded primary-source search verified the exact current 22/24 identifiers but did not locate their controlled outline, permissible pressure or full service/spring force curves. TSE's 90–120 psi caging instruction is not a maximum service-pressure rating. No assumed pressure times nominal chamber area is presented as an output bound. Compounding, pressure faults and the caliper's input-to-torque relationship remain required inputs.

The PAN17 drawing's 10,000 Nm maximum transferable mounting-interface torque is used only as a structural comparison case. It is not rated braking performance, an actuator maximum, a torque limiter or an established required proof load. The note calls for six M14 × 1.5 class 10.9 screws, at least 18 mm engagement and 205–225 Nm tightening torque. It does not qualify custom joints. Its 330 mm disc reference also needs reconciliation with the 325.12 mm Webb rotor.

The rotor torque path is axle → custom keyed hub/reducer → bolted rotor → pads. The reaction path is pads → OEM caliper bolts → custom axle-aligned carrier → single articulated torque link → new transverse frame crossmember → inner sideframe webs. It does not rely on the axlebox cover or route the link through the wheel web. Two rolling bearings support and align the carrier while the axle rotates; the link restrains carrier clock rotation. Loss of that active restraint can permit carrier rotation, hose damage and loss of controlled braking. A catcher is not a second functioning brake.

Bearing and finite-link screen

The first geometry uses bearing centres Y = [115, 175] mm, rotor force plane Y = 240 mm, carrier pin (600, 170, 450) mm and fixed frame pin (350, 170, 500) mm. The link is 254.951 mm long, but its nominal effective torque arm is only 147.087 mm.

The documented bearing candidate is NSK 6824DD, dimensions [120, 150, 16] mm, catalogue Cr = 32 kN and C0r = 35.5 kN. Catalogue evidence does not establish current inventory or an installed rating. A 120 mm bore permits passage over the nominal 110 mm wheel seat after wheel removal. The removable split 100-to-120 mm support sleeve, shoulders, fits and race-creep prevention remain custom design requirements. The earlier 100 mm bore cannot pass that seat; a nominal 110 mm bore does not establish assembly clearance.

Nominal results below use a 120 mm effective pad radius, zero heave/roll/lateral shift, one braking direction and zero added carrier mass:

High torque probes exceed the selected bearing's C0r reference. The short bearing span magnifies the overhung pad resultant. C0r is not a design allowable; values below it do not establish bearing life, shock capacity, combined-load acceptance or installation stiffness.

The solver closes the fixed link under axle heave, roll and lateral shift and balances all six force/moment components in carrier axes. Only the inboard station locates axially. Both torque signs are checked. The grid includes heave −40/−30/0/+35 mm, roll ±2°/0, lateral ±3/0 mm and additional journal-travel boundary states. The regular rows enforce both journal heights h ± 620 sin(roll) within −40 to +35 mm; accessible rows use −30 to +35 mm. Unrestricted combined rows deliberately retain overtravel sensitivities, while −60 mm is separately labeled captured extension. Thus h = +35 mm with roll = 2° is not presented as ordinary travel: one journal would rise 56.64 mm. These are vertical-journal-travel-admissible development screens, not full kinematic acceptance. The ±3 mm independent centre-lateral probe does not establish the retained locating/floating journal arrangement's actual lateral freedom.

Vertical-journal-travel-admissible regular-grid carrier clock ranges from -4.456° to 1.721°, with minimum effective torque arm 135.673 mm. The captured-extension minimum is 120.025 mm. Load probes also use effective radius 120/130/140 mm and 0/50 kg nonrotating mass at local CG [100, 198.982, 0] mm. The 50 kg value is not measured CAD mass or a certified upper bound. Rotor weight acts directly on the axle.

Current frame crossmember and link

The current member is HSS4x4x3/8 inch, section [101.6, 101.6, 9.525] mm, centred at [210, 0, 380] mm and supported at Y = [-526, 526] mm. It replaces the failed initial 3 × 3 candidate. The link anchor remains outside the member at [350, 170, 500] mm, so the real rising bracket introduces eccentric torque. Both same-axle brake reactions add. The screen uses the 0.93 wall factor, a rounded-corner sensitivity with outer radius three design walls, and symmetric torsional restraint at the member ends. That assumed end restraint still requires a real qualified load path.

The actual link has a 40 × 12 mm shank, 60 mm eye outside diameter and 35 mm Aurora GE20ES-2RS bearing seat. Clevises have 10 mm cheeks and 24 mm clear gap. The custom 20 mm headed pins are screened over the actual 34 mm reaction-centroid span, using a central point-load model for bending plus double shear. Custom pin material/heat treatment has not been specified, so no pin-strength pass is assigned.

Eye-net, projected-bearing and ideal Euler-shank checks are conditional local screens; they do not establish interference fit, race retention, breakout, oscillatory wear or fatigue life. Exact pin cross-hole, fillet and retention details also remain required.

The declared continuous 6 mm fillets receive a separate elastic weld-line screen. Each symmetric frame end transfers one brake force resultant and one bracket's torsional moment. The tube weld uses a rectangular perimeter approximation; the parent-web weld uses the declared endplate polygon and includes the 12 mm transfer offset. No weld allowable is assigned.

These are connection demands, not accepted weld capacities. Actual bead geometry, effective lengths, welding procedure, parent-wall load introduction, end torsional restraint and fatigue remain holds. The rotor-bolt and keyed-hub record separately gives ideal torque demand: two keys transfer torque in series, not as two parallel load-sharing keys. Their loaded lengths, bearing engagement and fatigue effect on the axle remain open. The 10 kNm comparison gives 12.766 kN tangential demand per rotor bolt under ideal equal sharing, and 200/166.667 kN at the 100/120 mm key interfaces respectively. No friction credit or keyway fatigue acceptance is assumed.

Service and acceptance holds

A caliper fixed to the upright axlebox would not follow axle tilt inside its journal bearing. At 2°, the rotor edge-plane mismatch is approximately 5.67 mm. A coaxial carrier follows the axle; its frame link must articulate through finite motion. The commercial wear slide is not itself angular accommodation.

Reserve actual pad extraction, adjuster access, full-wear caliper/chamber space, hoses, caging and removal. The family application guide describes approximately 25.4 mm wear movement; the caliper drawing gives 21 mm, so the larger reservation remains pending reconciliation. Rotor service requires supported wheelset removal, controlled wheel removal/reinstallation, runout and torque-joint inspection. Do not split or alter the OEM friction ring to bypass that procedure.

No joint strength, bearing or fatigue life, thermal duty, adhesion control, stopping distance or operating rating is assigned. Exact rotor/pad/chamber pairing, custom hub, carrier and frame attachments remain unresolved engineering interfaces.

Sources and reproduction

calculate_mechanics.py, carrier_statics.py, connection_screens.py and mechanics_report.py reproduce this note and mechanics-calculations.json using Python's standard library. The JSON records geometry snapshot hashes, sensitivity inputs and governing cases.

  • WEBB-R32 — Webb 55030PK-65: 325.12 mm diameter, 34.036 mm thickness, 148.59 mm overall depth, 168.91 mm pilot and eight M14 x 2 holes on 195.834 mm PCD. Internal profile and controlled hub mating-face offset remain open.
  • NSK-6824 — Selected thin bearing development candidate NSK 6824DD: 120 x 150 x 16 mm, Cr 32 kN, C0r 35.5 kN. Catalogue-only procurement evidence; no installed rating.
  • AISC-HSS — 0.93 nominal-wall convention for A500 section-property screening; not railway qualification.
  • NSK-6024 — Superseded, packaging-obstructed NSK 6024DDU comparison: 120 x 180 x 28 mm, Cr 97 kN, C0r 80 kN, 2,200 rpm and 2.08 kg. Catalogue ratings are not an installation acceptance.
  • H-L949 — PAN17 caliper, rotor, pad and current replacement chamber part numbers. Page 11 pad row says 20/24; page 15 current chamber row says 22/24; variant reconciliation remains open.
  • W-GA — 40175072/73 family mounting detail and 10,000 Nm transferable mounting-torque note. Drawing references 330 mm disc; exact 325 mm installation must be reconciled.
  • H-L1272 — Hendrickson 325 mm PAN17 package; chamber/caliper wear movement approximately 1 in and full suspension/hose clearance.
  • TSE-HOT — Public HOT family information; exact 4627/4628 GA and force envelope not found.
  • TSE-2026 — Current public family sheet; not an exact 22/24 force or dimensional record.
  • TSE-QR — Public options list; selected suffixes and full force envelope not given.
  • TSE-INSTALL — Disc-chamber service, caging access, lower drain and hose installation. Its 90–120 psi caging instruction is not a maximum working-pressure rating.

The nine exact-shape poses combine roll −2/0/+2° with ordinary rebound/mid/jounce. Centre heave is limited so both journal heights stay within−40..+35 mm. Their lateral translation preserves the right locating journal's Y coordinate; the left floating bearing takes the projection change. Matrices are in independent-motion-states.json. The wider analytical grid is identified separately as a development probe; Cartesian heave/roll overtravel is never called ordinary motion.

The first motion check found four real retainer-screw head clashes. Flush countersunk retainers removed those modeled interferences. The superseded evidence is retained in independent-motion-before-countersink.json; the final check is independent-motion-check.json.

The corrected barrel saddles have finite curved contact, and the outboard fork reaches the permanent barrel through a separate saddle bridge. The removable cover is relieved and is not credited as a welded load path. The bearing reducer now has actual external split shaft clamps; axial end screws alone were not treated as shaft clamping.

The hub's proposed axial restraint uses the closed shaft-key pocket, key ends, sleeve pocket wall and outboard retainer; two plates retain the hub to its sleeve. This explicitly needs key-end bearing, screw, fit, thermal-growth and axle-fatigue checks. No axial capacity is inferred merely from a key's torque function.

The 120 mm bearing/hub bores clear the nominal 110 mm wheel seat after the axlebox, journal nut/retainers and one wheel are removed. Actual fits, installation order, clamping, wheel-shop procedure and runout remain held. A closed inboard rotor is not replaceable on the coach with its wheel installed.

The exact Webb friction annulus/hat profile and true mounting datum,325.12 mm rotor versus330 mm caliper drawing compatibility, full TSE chamber/wear/caging envelope, mirrored brake coexistence and maximum actuator output remain unresolved. High-torque sensitivity failures in the mechanics report are not erased by these geometric checks.

C5.2 remains the historical failed tread-brake study. C5.3 is the current coach brake direction. No removed-brake mass saving or railway operating acceptance is credited.

C5.2 · R0 · 19 OCTOBER 2026

Tread brakes developed around available parts

Purchased-component-led tread-brake mechanism development, using an MGM Type 20/24 air-service / spring-parking chamber candidate and fabricated truck-mounted rigging. Nominal component fit and released suspension/flange-clearance checks fail. A compatible stock tread shoe, exact actuator interfaces and structural/fatigue qualification remain unresolved. Do not fabricate this candidate.

Open dimensioned mechanism drawings and actual-part CAD.

Purchased parts and fabrication boundary

The MGM 3420051 / TR2024 chamber has an active U.S. stock listing and an OEM two-day lead-time listing at retrieval. It is a development candidate, with the current product page and linked brochure disagreeing on mounting-stud thread. The chamber outline is a documented envelope; fittings, clamp projections, clevis engagement, caging tools and a verified pressure/force envelope remain separate interfaces. The fabricated supports and retained joints are project geometry. Catalog availability does not qualify the complete brake.

The current 600 mm wheel blank leaves only 80 mm of nominal tread outside the flange stock. The dimensioned IPS 24-inch shoe candidates are 84.074 or 90.424 mm wide and target a 609.6 mm wheel. They have not been selected, trimmed or treated as a match. Their mounting interfaces and wheel/friction compatibility must be established before a complete shoe head can be released.

Decisive motion finding

The suspended beam and actuator can close through their specified geometric travel, but that does not establish released shoe clearance. At the retained 40 mm regular-coach rebound, the reference shoe intrudes into the tread by 9.851 mm even with zero axle roll, lateral displacement and beam yaw. The 70 mm reference shoe also enters the flange region in the sampled axle-roll and lateral-motion screen; greater retraction alone does not resolve this. The reference insert is a failed fit study, not a selected purchased lining. A verified wheel/shoe pair and an effective release/tracking arrangement are prerequisites to further tread-brake detail. The independent review records the assumptions and governing cases.

The nominal CAD intersection audit also records unresolved component clashes. These are assembly-fit defects, separate from the released-shoe failure. The model shows the proposed parts and connection arrangement; it is not a verified fitted assembly. See the geometry record for the retained defects.

C5.2 conditional applied-contact mechanics — C52-GEOM-D2

UNRATED. Not released for fabrication or service. The applied-force model is useful for sizing, but it does not establish a working brake. Independent full-arc checks found released-head/tread-flange interference. No compatible stock shoe and finished wheel profile have been selected. The maximum credible MGM output is also unresolved. At the ordinary40 mm rebound,5 mm configured retraction and zero axle roll, lateral offset and beam yaw, the independent reference-face check finds 9.851 mm tread intrusion.

This report reads geometry-contract.json, hash 1e2bd575eb10ea37865334991f05845c975bbd5e92121ccf7b1285b01a26d833. calculate_mechanics.py reproduces the JSON and this note with Python, NumPy and SciPy. Geometry may subsequently strengthen or relocate a part; that change must be matched to a new calculation before a screen is reused.

What was calculated

The reference axle is X600/Z300 mm. One chamber operates two articulated heads through a guided crosshead, twin input links, a 250/100 mm crank and one output link. The output link attaches at the beam lug X270/Z300; its inclination produces a downward force as well as application force. At nominal geometry, horizontal beam force is 2.5 times chamber force and downward force is 0.384615 times chamber force.

The beam uses a candidate 8×3×1/4-inch HSS, 900 mm long, centred X200/Z300. The upper support is candidate 3×3×1/4-inch HSS. Grade C, supplier, availability and actual section properties remain procurement holds. Calculations use 0.93×6.35 = 5.9055 mm design wall, plus an assumed outer corner radius 3×nominal wall. Both bending inertias and rounded median torsion area are integrated. Holes, local brackets and weld notches are not included in the gross section.

Four fixed-length spherical links constrain the beam: three hangers and one lateral locator. Finite closure allows longitudinal application and small yaw; the beam therefore approximately equalizes the two contacts. It is not forced to carry exactly half the load at each head. Head seating uses distance perpendicular to one actual axle line. Journal travel at Y±620 and wheel-centre travel at Y±480 are recorded separately. Rotation outputs are rotation vectors, not Euler angles.

There are 180 applied-contact cases: three friction coefficients, both wheel-rotation directions and three input probes over ten geometry cases. These include ordinary heave cases and explicitly labelled captured-drop, axle-roll, split-stop and differential-wear sensitivities. They are not all approved operating states. A 75 kg moving-assembly gravity probe is included at reference X205/Z300, pending final CAD mass. Centering/return spring forces are not included because their parts and force curves are unresolved.

The six signed equilibrium unknowns are four link forces and two shoe normals. Maximum checked force residual is 4.7e-10 N; moment residual is 2.1e-09 N·mm. Negative link force means compression. All hangers must carry both signs. The circular head-resultant surrogate balances moment about the head pin; it does not prove lining pressure distribution, edge contact or the released shape.

Pressure and actuator limits

Type20 area × assumed pressure gives 8.896 kN at100 psi and11.121 kN at125 psi. These are theoretical sensitivities, not rated MGM output or a certified upper bound. The combined probe adds the published nominal1-inch spring point1280 lbf with its +10% tolerance: total17.384 kN input. Spring output below1 inch and maximum permitted operating pressure remain unverified. Anti-compounding is not established, so simultaneous service and spring application is explicitly probed. Efficiency is not credited to reduce structural load.

D2 motion from−5 to+15 mm, including yaw±0.3°, requires approximately 49.32 mm of the57.15 mm chamber stroke. That is only a linkage closure result. It does not clear the released shoe or establish a valid rod-cut/setup dimension. Depending on setup, nominal contact can occur below the first published1-inch spring point; that published value cannot be treated as the maximum spring force.

The JSON also contains11,000 kg and0.5 m/s² cases. Actual friction is a property of the selected block/wheel pair and conditions; it is not a chosen constant that establishes performance.

Conditional gross member screens

The HSS comparison is345/1.67 = 206.6 MPa, an elastic static screening convention, not railway acceptance criteria. Main-beam and upper-support values combine biaxial normal stress with a conservative sum of average shear and closed-cell torsional shear. Local wall bending, buckling, holes and welded-detail fatigue remain open. Upper-support end torsion is checked with0/50/100% distribution; actual connection stiffness is not asserted.

The frozen D2 63.5 mm shaft, with actual bearing centresY±205 mm, reaches 178.6 MPa before keyways and notches: below the 206.6 MPa gross-section comparison. The superseded50.8 mm candidate under the same loads and current support spacing reaches 345.6 MPa and is rejected by that comparison. The earlier305.8 MPa result used an assumed320 mm bearing span; current CAD has410 mm. Material, hubs, keys, bearing attachments and fatigue remain open. The revised shaft supports atY±205 require a new support-top-plate transition to the existing rails; that local connection is not qualified by this shaft screen. The comparison uses an assumed345 MPa yield; no unsupported1045 strength is claimed.

Peak absolute hanger forces are 9.31, 6.54 and 21.27 kN; lateral locator peak is0.807 kN. The third hanger's asymmetric location and the inclined driven link create a substantial pitch reaction. A55 mm eye with35 mm bearing bore in12 mm plate has a nominal net stress of88.6 MPa; machined grooves, fit, retention and eye stress concentration are not included.

The maximum pivot load is43.97 kN. A19.928 mm minimum-diameter screen with32 mm clevis support-centroid span gives452.8 MPa bending plus70.5 MPa double-shear stress, or468.9 MPa VM. Bending is not omitted. This assumes full shoulder material through both shear planes; actual grip, shoulder fillets, tightening limit, retention and fatigue remain to be matched. The nominal pin stack is16 mm bearing inner ring + two4 mm spacers + two8 mm cheeks +2.5 mm washer =42.5 mm, exceeding the40.25 mm maximum shoulder by2.25 mm; the20.5 mm washer bore clears the shoulder tip. Nominal bottoming is addressed, while controlled fits, tightening and locking remain HOLD. The sixM4 eye-retainer screws retain the outer race and are not assigned the primary brake-force path. Catalogue bearing radial static limit146 kN is not a fabricated-joint allowable load.

Proposed weld details and remaining load path

The following are proposed paths accepted as candidate metadata only; final CAD has fit-up bolts but no completed webpatch/weld connection, using6 mm E70 fillets and an elastic line-weld model. The HSS-end screen credits only straight flats, excluding rounded corner arcs. Endplate-to-sideweb paths run along X140..285/Z350, X140/Z350..440 and X285/Z350..525. No weld is credited at the free top edgeZ550, which lies above the sideweb. The HSS weld planes areY±526 and the web planesY±538. The endplate-to-web screen includes the12 mm offset between those planes. Upper-member bending uses the1052 mm actual tube-end span.

Proposed jointPeak elastic weld traction, MPaStatic comparison, MPa
upper_HSS_to_endplate69.4144.8

These weld figures cover the upper hanger support reactions only. They do not establish the actuator bracket, crankshaft-bearing supports, hanger clevis-to-HSS local walls or sideframe fatigue capacity. Actual weld access, fit-up, filler/WPS, local plate bending and member wall checks remain necessary. The mechanism must not be described as having a complete verified mounting load path while these joints are open.

Blocking cases and fatigue

A single head contacting while the other is absent or held clear is a different load path. The combined probe requires21.3 kN·m of yaw restraint; a900 mm restraint couple would carry23.7 kN. A simple full-force single-head bending probe gives270.3 MPa in the main beam, above the elastic comparison. No capacity is assigned to the stops or catchers. A drop catcher does not preserve a functioning brake after a reaction-path failure.

The reported stress levels are not a fatigue-life result. The JSON includes nominal reversal-range upper bounds, but no accepted weld category, stress concentration, service cycle count or damage sum. Repeated service stops, reverse-direction braking, persistent parking load, shocks and released-head interference need separate treatment. The present released-head interference and incompatible stock shoe/wheel interface are blocking findings, regardless of favourable gross-member numbers.
