Wheel material, manufacturing and fit basis

Custom wheels, axles and brake attachment — fabrication development

Revision PF-TRAIN-S3L-WHEEL-R0 • Reviewed 19 October 2026 UTC / 18 October 2026 Nevada time. Preliminary engineering for a fabrication package; production dimensions are not released.

What we will build

Develop a matched rigid wheelset: two custom-machined monobloc rail wheels on one engineered rotating axle, carried by bearings in the bogie. Start with a qualified railway wheel blank, with the flange formed as part of that wheel. The farm can build much of the surrounding vehicle; the wheel profile, wheel/axle fits, heat treatment and wheelset pressing belong with a rail wheel machine shop.

The brake connection is a designed part of this assembly. Develop either H-01A, a bolted disc on a machined integral wheel-hub mounting face, or H-01B, a separate engineered disc carrier on an axle seat. Do not weld a generic rotor carrier to an axle, drill an arbitrary bolt circle into a finished wheel, or assume a clamp collar can carry braking torque. The brake architecture team must choose one interface and close its torque, temperature, fatigue and service-access requirements before we finish the axle and wheel drawings.

This is a genuine custom manufacturing route: ORX's wheel page ↗ specifically describes machining wheel hubs for bolted brake discs and specialized profiles, and its wheelset page ↗ explicitly includes narrow gauge. Penn Machine ↗ offers custom forged solid wheels and design analysis. No supplier has yet accepted our dimensions, order size, application or delivery date. [WF01, WF03, WF04]

What carries forward

Phase 3G identified its wheel, axle and bearing shapes as symbols, not accepted hardware. Phase 3K used 0.25 and 0.30 m rolling radii only as calculations. Neither supplied a fabrication-ready wheel.

Retain the 36 in / 914.4 mm gauge evaluation, four-axle coach analysis and 32 in raised-floor study. Gauge alone determines neither wheel back-to-back nor axle length. The rail head, wheel contact profile, flange gauge point, turnout check gauge and flangeway all need a coordinated drawing. We must check new and worn geometry, tolerance extremes and lateral motion. Smaller wheels also change brake packaging, contact stress and rotational speed; a blank's availability does not establish suitability.

Loads already available to put on the engineering drawings

These are balanced, level-track analytical cases, not an accepted wheel/axle rating. Gravity is 9.80665 m/s².

CaseCoach massStatic load per axleStatic load per wheel
Regular, upper screen13,200 kg32.362 kN16.181 kN
Regular, allocated tare7,040 kg17.260 kN8.630 kN
Accessible, upper screen11,000 kg26.968 kN13.484 kN

For a regular coach with four equally braked axles, F_axle = m × a / 4 and T_axle = F_axle × D / 2:

Rolling-diameter probeTorque/axle at 0.5 m/s²Torque/axle at 0.7 m/s²Speed at 7.5 mph
500 mm412.5 N·m577.5 N·m128.1 rpm
600 mm495.0 N·m693.0 N·m106.7 rpm

These demands exclude rotational inertia and unequal brake distribution. With equal left/right contact, each wheel contributes half the axle torque; a single axle disc and its carrier still transmit the whole axle torque. The joint and mount also require the maximum physically achievable brake torque, including parking application and credible simultaneous service/parking faults. Designing them only for the table would miss an important load case.

Before sizing the axle add the bearing-to-contact-point offsets, actual sprung/unsprung mass, lateral flange forces, track twist, load transfer, braking forces, impact/load factors and lifetime cycle spectrum. Existing coach-frame acceleration studies do not automatically define the correct wheelset fatigue loading.

The accompanying dimension matrix lists each held item explicitly. It does not disguise an unknown bore, fit or flange geometry as a shop dimension.

Material and process route

Preferred development route: forged/wrought monobloc wheel with integral flange, and a one-piece forged, heat-treated axle. ASTM A504/A504M-18(2023) ↗ is an active wheel specification covering material classes, treatment, hardness and inspection. ASTM A729/A729M-17(2022) ↗ covers heat-treated rail axles, including material/mechanical testing and nondestructive examination. These are candidate technical specifications, not asserted local legal requirements. We reviewed their public scope/abstract; the engineer and shop must obtain and use the full adopted specification. [WF06, WF07]

Do not select a wheel class solely because the train is slow, or specify ordinary 4140 bar as equivalent to a qualified railway axle. The required class, cleanliness, mechanical properties, treatment, fatigue performance and inspection must travel together. ORX's ability to machine several alloy families is manufacturing capability, not our material approval. [WF05]

Alternative: a qualified cast-steel rail wheel from a wheel manufacturer, with its own design and quality route. Amsted's published standard one-wear sizes start at 28 in, larger than the present 500/600 mm probes; its catalog is not evidence of a fitting small wheel. [WF10] A flame-cut plate wheel with a welded flange is not an established alternative in this package.

How the wheel shop makes and assembles it

  1. Freeze interfaces: wheel/rail profile, diameter and worn limits, bearing arrangement, back-to-back, brake attachment and removal sequence.
  2. Release drawings and process requirements: wheel/axle detail and assembly drawings, material/class, treatment, tolerances, NDT and acceptance records.
  3. Buy traceable blanks: approved forging/casting source and stock allowance; heat/lot and material certificates.
  4. Rough-machine and treat: follow the approved sequence; preserve final-machining stock; record treatment and test results.
  5. Finish-machine: profile and fit surfaces, transitions, runout and finish; inspect and record each serialized part.
  6. Match and press: measure each bore and seat, control their temperature, use approved tooling/lubricant and record force versus displacement. Reject/disposition an unacceptable fit; welding or set screws are not a remedy.
  7. Install bearings and discs: follow the selected interface and OEM instructions; inspect retention, preload/locking, runout and free rotation.
  8. First-article acceptance: dimensional record, instrumented brake torque and hot/cold interface checks, full movement clearance and engineer disposition before production.

This proposed traveler is supported by railway manufacturing practice. APTA's wheelset assembly standard ↗ addresses measured fits, pressing records and assembly qualification. Its large-axle tabulations must not be scaled into a guessed fit for this train. Its axle design practice, Rev. 2 ↗, provides a useful framework for seats, transitions, material/process control and fatigue. Responsible engineering must choose a coherent technical basis and confirm applicability. [WF08, WF09]

Calculations that turn the held fields into machining dimensions

For a preliminary solid circular axle section, nominal bending stress is 32M/(πd³) and nominal torsional stress is 16T/(πd³). These are starting equations only. Local transitions, press-fit fretting, surface condition, residual stress, corrosion and alternating loads determine fatigue suitability; a yield-strength check alone cannot release A-01.

For an ideal cylindrical friction fit, T = μ p π d² L / 2 and F_axial = μ p π d L. Actual interference requires shaft/hub elastic compatibility, material properties, geometry and hot/cold fit conditions. Unknown friction, hub stiffness and contact pressure are not substituted with convenient values. A wheel web and a short carrier can produce nonuniform pressure requiring a more complete model. Press force is a measured assembly acceptance variable, not an interchangeable torque rating.

Farm-crew scope now: build non-load-bearing fit mockups and protective handling aids to reviewed drawings; assist with later installation and inspection records under qualified supervision. Rail specialist scope: blanks, thermal processing, precision wheel/axle machining, NDT, pressing and wheelset dimensional acceptance. The custom brake bracket and bogie weldments can become local fabrication work after their calculations, drawings, weld procedures and inspection plan are released.

Next concrete decision: select the complete foundation brake and wheelset interface together, then freeze W-01/A-01/H-01 machining geometry. Do not order unmachined blanks until the wheel supplier confirms a viable blank and material/process route for that geometry.

Caliper source drawings, ratings and exact-variant questions

Actual brake interface for the custom wheelset study

Checked 19 October 2026 UTC. These are manufacturer dimensions and a proposed fit study, not a released passenger-rail brake assembly.

Primary fit-study candidate: Tolomatic ME220MEFI, part 0745-0024. This is a cast-iron mechanical caliper with machined cam and floating bracket for a fixed 1/2-inch disc. It provides a physical component around which the custom carrier can be drawn while preserving the owner's preference for a commercial combination air/spring chamber. The chamber, force-reduction linkage and brake are not a documented matched assembly.

Verified interface

The OEM floating bracket has two Ø0.386-inch holes at 4.750-inch centers. Draw those two OEM holes at datum coordinates H1=(0,0), H2=(4.750,0) inches. The new carrier's hole clearance, thickness, edge distance, threads, bolts and preload are separate design decisions. The OEM bracket is 7.00 inches wide and 2.92 inches high; its mounting-hole line is 2.17 inches above the lever-axis line. The dimension sheet lists the candidate's A/B/C dimensions as 0.844/3.80/5.98 inches. These are catalogue dimensions, not assigned machining tolerances.

For a 12-inch-diameter packaging study, the OEM table gives D=5.00 inches from disc axis to lever axis and effective braking radius=5.08 inches. Thus the mounting-hole line is 7.17 inches from the axle axis in the depicted radial orientation. This does not define the axial mounting-face datum: use the exact manufacturer CAD or controlled drawing before fixing the carrier standoff. The caliper floating motion accommodates its brake mechanism, not unrestricted wheelset suspension travel.

Sources: ME220 product ↗, OEM dimensional sheet ↗.

Verified force/travel information and integration consequences

The OEM specifications distinguish 580 lbf maximum lever force for aluminum housings and 660 lbf for cast iron. They give dynamic torque in inch-pounds as 7.45 × braking radius in inches × lever force in pounds; static torque uses coefficient 3.725. New total pad clearance is 0.048 inch; maximum axial movement without intermediate adjustment is 0.387 inch; wear before adjustment is 0.104 inch per side. The printed angular-stroke notation requires clarification before conversion.

The drawing shows 4.00-inch and 2.38-inch lever-hole radii. Confirm which input location the catalogue equation covers. Do not apply the rated equation unchanged after moving the pushrod to a different hole. The linked parts sheet explicitly covers die-cast aluminum and gives 580 lbf; this is not a conflict with the cast-iron catalogue rating. Obtain the exact cast-iron variant instructions before accepting 660 lbf in this assembly. It also confirms that wear adjustment is manual.

Sources: OEM specifications ↗, linked parts sheet ↗.

Design implication: a common 30/30 chamber must not be bolted directly to this lever on the assumption that its force is suitable. Match full pressure, maximum parking-spring force, travel, wear and simultaneous service/parking failure loads. A reduction linkage or different chamber may be necessary. Carrier loads include disc reactions and actuator-linkage loads, not only the demanded stopping torque.

Use an axlebox-referenced carrier concept, or another proven means of preserving disc/caliper alignment during primary suspension motion. A torque link may react braking into the bogie, but its pivots, geometry and joint forces remain to be engineered. A rigid chassis bracket with no suspension accommodation is not an acceptable continuation of the current sketch.

A measured alternative, not a change in preference

Twiflex MX13/MX25 calipers accept documented proprietary air thrusters. MXA supplies air-applied service braking; MXK is spring-applied and air-released. DS2501 lists MXA maximum tangential braking force 6.9 kN at 7 bar, based on conditioned pads at nominal friction 0.4. With a 300 mm disc and the manufacturer's effective-radius formula, this implies 807.3 N·m. This is a published operating-condition estimate, not an ultimate carrier load or rail qualification. MXK legacy and current documents differ slightly on the spring force, requiring reconciliation. Neither is a verified substitute for an MGM canister.

Sources: MXA DS2501 ↗, current MX mounting sheet ↗.

The next fabrication development can now include a real mounting pattern, candidate axial envelope, removable carrier arrangement, inspection-access space, custom wheel/axle/rotor interface schedule and force-based carrier calculation. Rotor material, thermal capacity, hub attachment, bearings, axle and wheel fits, fatigue loads, tolerances and brake balance remain coordinated engineering decisions. No stock or application approval has been confirmed, and no supplier has been contacted.

Loading installation study…

Understand what is shown

The 32-inch floor is a study input. Colored boxes reserve space; they are not selected equipment or verified mounting details. The wheel cylinders remain at rest and omit the complete bogie. Deployed stairs are shown only to understand stopped boarding space; no stowed mechanism or moving clearance is established.

Installation reservations

Equipment space, maintenance withdrawal space and routing corridors serve different purposes. Maintenance volumes do not travel with the vehicle. None is a verified component fit.

The orange shapes are nominal cylinders, not an approved wheel profile or a complete bogie. Static views do not establish full-motion clearance. All frame joints, configured running gear and fabrication release remain open.

Read the calculations and mockup instructions →

Compare the motion assumptions

These are calculated trial envelopes, not measured suspension limits. Changing the case below updates the table only; the rotating models remain straight-track cylinders at rest.

Five sampled yaw positions between ±5.247° for a 30 m curve study. The larger-radius cylinders are deliberately simplified envelopes, not actual wheel flanges. Required clearance, wheel profile and suspension motion remain supplier/engineer inputs.

One coordinated load basis

Regular coach upper calculation case: 13,200 kg. Accessible: 11,000 kg. Complete train: 65,100 kg including the existing locomotive/hotel allowance.

These are proposed engineering cases. Target tare and nominal passenger load budgets are unchanged. The track design target remains 0% longitudinal grade.

PHASE 3A · R0 · 17 OCTOBER 2026

The engineering behind the build.

Read the complete technical release below. Nine subsystem sheets explain the load paths, assemblies and interfaces. Component ratings are separated from calculated loads and unresolved details.

Current owner direction

  • 0% longitudinal grade. Finished elevations must be verified; drainage remains part of the civil design.
  • Passengers disembark at the North Pole. Its location will be supplied later.
  • Maximize usable interior space and develop a practical farm-crew track installation scope.

What this issue provides

Frame load paths, brake and coupling calculations, real component candidates, body and boarding assemblies, hotel-power architecture, installation sequence and acceptance gates.

Final manufacturing dimensions, welds, rated assemblies and control circuits require the responsible engineers and manufacturers. This release is not approved for fabrication.

Technical source package ↓

CURRENT STUDY BASIS

Dimensions with their limits.

These values coordinate the current study. They are not released manufacturing dimensions or supplier ratings.

Performance screens

  • Nominal loaded train: 54,755 kg.
  • 145 kg/person stress case: 59,840 kg. Phase 3B upper calculation case: 65,100 kg; not a rated train mass.
  • Reference calculation speed: 7.5 mph; operating speed remains to be established.
  • The owner requires 0% longitudinal grade. Verify finished elevations; curve, adhesion and braking limits still depend on actual equipment.

Requirements retained

  • Diesel propulsion beneath historical styling.
  • Heating, air conditioning, ventilation and enclosed bodies.
  • Bench seating without tables or armrests.
  • Onboard end platforms and protected stairs.
  • CR-G01: air service brakes and spring-applied, air-released parking are preferred. Common semi-truck combination chambers are inquiry candidates; the complete rail brake system remains to be engineered.

Masses are allocations, not measured equipment weights. Mobility devices are additional to the counted users. Dimensions, capacity and mass must change together when a design decision changes.

BUILD PACKAGE STATUS

What you can review. What still needs design.

The 3L.1 issue adds custom wheelset and brake-carrier interfaces, machining development and a complete fabrication drawing register. Phase 3K adds trainwide air-brake functions, real component evidence, stopping and air budgets, and verification requirements. Phase 3J adds a coordinated J1a member model, global load-path calculations, representative prototype and inspection records. Phase 3I adds dimensioned connection alternatives, reinforced seat/floor attachments, local load-path calculations and assembly records. Phase 3H adds named frame parts, grouped cuts, material candidates, located connections, calculated joint trials, floor panels and revised seat-support coordinates. Phase 3G adds pivoting bogie motion and the CR-G01 air-brake direction. Phase 3F adds first-coach installation, boarding, connection and systems interfaces with a controlled fabrication sequence. Phase 3E adds clearance-resolution studies, full-size ground-level mockup instructions and supplier/prototype verification packets. Phase 3D retains lighter-frame comparisons, floor-span studies, running-gear fit and brake/power interfaces. Phase 3C retains the assembly sections and fabrication records. Phase 3B mockups and the Phase 3A engineering basis remain available. Use the distinction below to see which work is developed and which details still require engineering release.

Show what you can deliver.

BEFORE FABRICATION RELEASE

Evidence needed to close the gaps.

No unresolved safety-critical detail is made complete by a drawn dimension. Close each applicable hold through the responsible engineering and approval process.

TECHNICAL REVIEW FILES

Use the source geometry.

Read the documents in the browser or download the files needed for your review. The technical package contains the CAD geometry and its stated limitations.

CAD formats and units

Phase 3I: local connection STEP solids use millimetres; browser meshes and canonical geometry use inches. Python retains editable parameters. Dimensions describe preliminary options, with final hardware, weld procedures and tolerances held where evidence is incomplete. Each drawing and model README defines its format limits.

Phase 3H: STEP solids use millimetres; part meshes and source coordinates use inches. Frame members carry nominal section dimensions and material candidates. HSS corners are simplified; tube manufacturing geometry, end preparation, holes and final connections remain unreleased. Drawing DXFs distinguish paper-inch layouts from actual-inch details.

Phase 3G: five articulated bogie STEP poses use millimetres; browser meshes use inches. Visible support, suspension and wheel hardware is functional display geometry. Read the report for CR-G01 air service and spring parking; old hydraulic installation boxes are superseded.

Phase 3F: STEP installation solids use millimetres; browser meshes use inches. Colored volumes are space reservations, with F1 at a 32 in study floor. Deployed stair surfaces do not define stowage or manufacturing thickness. Phase 3E retains the alternative floor/frame comparisons.

Phase 3C assemblies: STEP solids use millimetres; browser meshes use inches. Member/panel studies omit final joints and actual running gear. Phase 3B mockups: STEP solids use millimetres; browser meshes and input coordinates use inches. Ground-level interior envelopes only; no native feature tree or released manufacturing model. Read each drawing README for sheet versus model-space units.

Phase 3: sheet DXFs use paper inches; the separate coach-interface-envelope DXF uses model inches. SVG/PDF sheets are engineering diagrams, not production geometry. Retained Stage 2 files:

DXF: editable 2D plans in inches. STEP: 3D solid space envelopes in millimetres. SVG: vector plans. Python retains the authoring parameters; JSON records exported values and is not read back automatically. STEP does not carry a native CAD feature tree.

Retained earlier reference: 216 in bogie centres, 28 in floor above rail and 44 in end platforms. Phase 3F explicitly studies a 32 in floor without approving that change. No actual bogie, frame member, wheel profile, coupler pin, weld, material grade or rated capacity is selected by the drawings.
